On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 14 v1.0

# The Necessity Form of Field-Concretization Qualification

1\. Purpose  
This paper is a Necessity Form. Its use: under a given host structure, dynamical law and condition group C, to prove that the three duties of Sedimentation-Field qualification, TraceSet, Part and HDyn, cannot fail to hold simultaneously on one and the same declared Trace-Response set T, so that the fact of constitution Fld cannot fail to hold; the form is C ⇒ R. Every item of the condition group is only a constraint on declared law coefficients, the alphabet, the initial state and the input sequence; verifying it takes only arithmetic or inspection of the letter sequence, runs no overall dynamics and adjudicates no duty.  
The central problem is this. *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* gives a verdict on one declared instance (one member, one complete history, one window); the cost of its decision procedure grows exponentially with window length in the worst case, and finite enumeration states no conclusion over “a class of members × all subsequent inputs.” Proposition 1 of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* gives a necessary and sufficient closed form only on the linear F-layer families and for a single history shape, and there HistCond is supplied directly by linear laws or relaxation that read their own position. In hosts of the event-gating class (of which spiking neural networks are representative), instances that satisfy the antecedents of Proposition N1 or N2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* (such as Instance P of that paper) cannot be faithfully encoded in the F layer (that paper does not prove that every member of 𝒦\_g is unencodable); in the subclass of this paper, the processes acting on the Trace-Response set are linked to the Trace-Responses already left by threshold gating and by no other route. On such hosts, which conditions make qualification hold universally, for arbitrary subsequent input and every window containing a qualifying probe, has not been answered upstream. This paper answers it, and gives the tightness of each condition, the formation threshold, regime extensions, stochastic quantifiers and the counterpart form on the gated extension classes.  
The conclusions of this paper fall into seven groups.  
　1\. Main theorem: the self-sustainment–pivot theorem 14-1⁺ on the subclass 𝒦\_LS of the event-gating class, the full table of the condition group C\_LS⁺ (4.3), the dynamics lemmas 14.1–14.7 and certificate probes (Section 5, 6.1).  
　2\. Two readings and the source: two sufficient ranges for E11 at clipping (Proposition 14-4); the source condition F3ᵤ at the first pairing beat and the blocking construction outside it (Proposition 14-5) (6.2, 6.3).  
　3\. Tightness and equivalence characterization: K3 cannot be relaxed (Proposition 14-2), K2′ is equivalent to self-sustainment (Proposition 14-3), the exact formation threshold for a given spacing sequence (Proposition 14-6), the same-family equivalence characterization (Theorem 14-7) (6.4).  
　4\. Regime extension and stochastic quantifiers: certificate probes cover windows without a clean probe and windows with several probes; a probability lower bound and an almost-sure version under i.i.d. input (Proposition 14-8) (6.5).  
　5\. Gated extension classes: the FD-level conditional theorem 14-9 on a three-gate design in 𝒦\_PA^≺ of Chapter 13 (6.6).  
　6\. The linear own-position family and the non-neural side: Lemma 14-L, a structural observation on the linear own-position family, the formal-layer witness and budget obstruction of the threshold-gated reading Family 1ᵍ, and the open route of bilinear-partner support (6.7).  
　7\. Boundary and structural-collapse conditions, model-layer witnesses and finite verification, conditional predictions for real hosts, summary formulas, strength, open items and interfaces (Sections 7–13).  
Status of the conclusions:  
　The conclusion of every theorem is a universal statement “Conditional on the listed conditions and the antecedents consumed”; anyone downstream who obtains a duty or Fld through this paper carries along the listed conditions, the read-back antecedents and the H group. ¬C does not imply ¬Fld.  
　“R witness = 1” means that the appendix programs construct positive witnesses for q, r, Part and HDyn under the atom criteria of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*; a witness not obtained is recorded as None and is not read as 0; ⊥ does not imply 0.  
　Model verdicts of 1 and FD-level conclusions belong only to Column A and are not certification of any real host (biological neural tissue, neuromorphic hardware, material hosts).  
Preconditions of adjudication:  
(1) The duties and the fact of constitution take the formal master formula of Section 5 and Corollary 1 of *The General Form of Field Concretization*; on 𝒦\_g the atoms a, η, κ, q, r and the verdicts take 3.4 and 4.2–4.4 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*.  
(2) The host laws take Definitions 1–3 of 3.1 (members, beat-wise execution order, general shape and closure) and Definition 5 of 3.2 (individuation of events) of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*; the gated extension classes take Definitions 5 and 7 and Proposition PA-2 of 3.6 of *The Adjudication Form of Material Field Concretization*.  
(3) Read-back takes Lemmas 3.1 and 3.2 of 5.2, Theorem D2 of 5.3, Theorem COR of 5.4 and Theorem R7 of 5.5 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*, together with the antecedents listed in its condition ledger in 7.2.  
Three columns kept apart: Column A holds model conclusions (all of Sections 4–8 belong to A); Column B holds conditional predictions for real hosts (Section 9); Column C holds empirical certification (none). The three columns do not substitute for one another.  
Theoretical increment: upstream supplies a decision procedure and read-back lemmas; this paper supplies the conditions under which qualification “cannot fail to occur.” The load-bearing new content is the universal dynamical part: the self-sustainment invariant (Lemma 14.4), the membrane-potential upper bound with a strictness flag, computed from the letter sequence alone, and certificate probes (Lemma 14.7), the threshold-gating pivot (Lemma 14.5), together with the exact threshold of each condition (Propositions 14-2, 14-3, 14-6) and the same-family equivalence characterization (Theorem 14-7). None of this is upstream, and none of it can be obtained from the finite verdicts upstream (6.8).

3\. Core Proposition  
Let G be a member of the event-gating class 𝒦\_g, with target gating unit j and probe entry set A declared, and T := {(i, j) : i ∈ A}. Suppose the law coefficients satisfy effective potentiation (K1), self-sustainment (K2′) and a subthreshold baseline after rest (K3); the initial state is resting and the formation regime yields enough Trace-Response (F1 and the formation-amount condition); and an admissible removal exists at the first pairing beat (F3ᵤ). Then for every subsequent letter sequence satisfying the input regime (length, letters and nonnegative teaching current arbitrary, input to the other units arbitrary) and every window W containing a certificate probe, TraceSet, Part and HDyn hold simultaneously on T, and Fld holds (algorithmic level, model mode). The antecedents consumed are the read-back antecedents listed in 7.2 of Chapter 11; when no certificate probe in the window satisfies NS\*, the individuation of clipping E11 is carried in addition. The mechanism: through its own action on the gate, the Trace-Response makes every beat with A as input fire, which keeps the depression component below the potentiation component (self-sustainment); after rest the baseline is not enough to cross the threshold, so setting T back to baseline at the probe beat closes the gate and cancels potentiation (the pivot). Each coefficient condition is an exact threshold (Propositions 14-2, 14-3, 14-6), and together, under the background antecedents, they give the same-family equivalence characterization (Theorem 14-7).

4\. Operator Mapping

### 4.1  Working Domain and Instance

Subclass 𝒦\_LS(j, A) ⊆ 𝒦\_g: G ∈ 𝒦\_g (Definition 1 of Chapter 11) satisfying the items of □C\_hold in 4.3. Instance χ = χ(G, h, W): P is the program G; L is the discrete beats of the algorithmic level, with resolution ε; B is all entry units, gating units and synapses; W = \[t\_s, t\_e\]; the out-of-boundary signals are the entry stimuli and teaching currents in the letter sequence h (in the format of Definition 4 of Chapter 11). Model mode.

### 4.2  Mapping Table

　Host objects → operators and quantities of Chapter 11  
　　member G, state o = (w, v, x, y), letter σ = (a, I), entry pattern s = pat(a) → Definitions 1–2;  
　　beat-wise execution order (read in; integrate, vt\_j = λ\_j v\_j + Σ\_i w\_ij s\_i + I\_j; gate, g\_j = \[vt\_j ≥ θ\_j\]; reset; eligibility traces, x′ = d\_x x + s, y′ = d\_y y + g; potentiation PLUS\_ij = A₊ x′\_i (when g\_j = 1); depression MINUS\_ij = −A₋ y′\_j (when s\_i = 1); relaxation RELAX\_ij = −ρ(w\_ij − b\_ij); clipping CLIP to \[lo\_ij, hi\_ij\]) → Definition 2; potentiation and depression depend only on (x′, y′, g, s) and do not read w, and relaxation reads only the w at its own position → Definition 3;  
　　the events LEAK, SYN, INJ, RESET, YIN, PLUS, MINUS, RELAX, CLIP and their read sets → Definition 5 (the read set of PLUS contains the gating read set);  
　　Trace-Response z\_ij := w\_ij − b\_ij; declared Trace-Response set T := {(i, j) : i ∈ A}, given by the structural declaration and independent of the trajectory;  
　　y\_sup := 1/(1 − d\_y,j), x\_sup,i := 1/(1 − d\_x,i) (when d \< 1).  
　Properties of letter sequences  
　　rest beat for j: the entry pattern is all 0 and I\_j = 0 (the teaching current of other gating units may be nonzero);  
　　pairing beat: the entry pattern contains all entries of A and I\_j ≥ θ\_j;  
　　probe beat: the entry pattern is exactly the indicator vector of A and I\_j = 0;  
　　m-clean probe beat: the m beats immediately preceding the probe beat are all rest beats for j;  
　　certificate probe beat: see the definition in 5.3 (decided from the letter sequence and the declared parameters alone).  
　Duties and atoms  
　　TraceSet (q, r), Part, HDyn (Act, HistCond), Fld → Section 5 of *The General Form of Field Concretization*;  
　　on 𝒦\_g: a(e), FD\_𝒟₁(e), Path\_act, η(e), κ, q, r\_R and the verdicts → 3.4 and 4.2–4.4 of Chapter 11; read-back → Lemmas 3.1, 3.2 and Theorems D2, COR, R7.  
　　NS*(t*) :⇔ ∃ i ∈ A, hi\_ij − w\_ij(t\*) \> ε (at the certificate probe beat some T position is more than the resolution below its upper bound).  
4.3  Condition Group C\_LS⁺ (full table; source and method of verification item by item)  
□C\_hold (law coefficients, protocol, alphabet; unchanged over the whole history)  
　P0  plastic = bap = 1. Source: the declared protocol switches (Definition 1 of Chapter 11). Verification: read the declaration.  
　S1  lo\_ij ≥ 0 (for every entry i). Source: the declared valid intervals. Verification: read the declaration.  
　S2  v\_reset,j = 0, θ\_j \> 0, λ\_j ≤ 1. Source: the declared law coefficients. Verification: read the declaration.  
　S3  ρ = 0 (no relaxation toward the baseline). Source: the declared law coefficients. Verification: read the declaration.  
　S4  The alphabet is A-indecomposable (the entry pattern of every letter is all 1 or all 0 on A) and contains a probe letter that is exactly A. Source: the declared alphabet. Verification: inspect pat.  
　K1  A₊ \> ε. Source: the law coefficients and the declared resolution. Verification: arithmetic.  
　K2′  A₋ = 0, or d\_y,j \< 1 and A₊(1 − d\_y,j) ≥ A₋. Source: the law coefficients. Verification: arithmetic.  
　K3(m)  λ\_j^(m+1)·θ\_j + Σ\_{i∈A} b\_ij ≤ θ\_j, and Σ\_{i∈A} b\_ij \< θ\_j (when λ\_j \> 0 the latter follows from the former). Source: the law coefficients, the baselines and the declared rest margin m. Verification: arithmetic.  
　R1  Every letter belongs to the declared alphabet, and I ∈ ℚ\_{≥0}^{n\_out}. Source: the declared input regime. Verification: inspect the letter sequence.  
C\_init (initial resources and formation regime)  
　I0  The declared initial state is the resting state o₀ = (b, 0, 0, 0). Verification: read the declaration.  
　F1  From beat 0 to the n-th pairing beat p\_n, every beat is a pairing beat or a rest beat for j; between adjacent pairing beats there are at least m\_F rest beats. Verification: inspect the letter sequence.  
　Formation-amount condition, one of the following three (derived quantities in 4.4):  
　　(i) F0 ∧ F2;  
　　(ii) F0 ∧ F2′;  
　　(iii) Φ(g) ≥ θ\_j and min\_{i∈A}(w\_i(p\_n + 1; g) − b\_ij) \> ε, where g is the actual pairing spacing sequence.  
　F3ᵤ  F3 ∨ F3\* ∨ F3° (4.5). Verification: arithmetic on baselines, reset values, thresholds and letters.  
Window conditions  
　R2⁺  p\_n \< t\_s, and W contains a certificate probe beat t\* (5.3). R2 (W contains an m-clean probe beat) is a special case of R2⁺ (Lemma 14.7′). Verification: inspect the letter sequence and arithmetic.  
H group  
　H\_IND  E11 of 7.2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* (individuation of clipping, separate reading), consumed only when NS\* fails at every certificate probe beat in W (Proposition 14-4).  
Read-back antecedents (carried with the conclusion): the items listed in 7.2 of Chapter 11 for positive determinations of TraceSet, Part and HDyn and of Fld—E1–E8, E12, E14, E15, E25, E26, the reading clause E21, and Theorem COR.  
Shorthand convention: below, wherever “C\_LS⁺” or “the formation regime satisfies …” is written, it means the full table of this section, including F3ᵤ, H\_IND and the read-back antecedents.  
4.4  Derived Quantities and the Formation Recursion  
　δ\_F := A₊ − A₋·Ȳ\_F, Ȳ\_F := 1/(1 − d\_y,j^(m\_F+1)) (δ\_F := A₊ when A₋ = 0). F0: δ\_F \> 0.  
　ω\_i := min(hi\_ij, b\_ij + n·δ\_F). F2: (a) Σ\_{i∈A} ω\_i ≥ θ\_j; (b) min\_{i∈A}(ω\_i − b\_ij) \> ε.  
　ρ\_F := d\_y,j^(m\_F+1), Ȳ\_k := (1 − ρ\_F^k)/(1 − ρ\_F) (Ȳ\_k = k when ρ\_F = 1). F2′: (a) and (b) with ω′\_i := min(hi\_ij, b\_ij + Σ\_{k=1}^{n} (A₊ − A₋Ȳ\_k)) in place of ω\_i.  
　Formation recursion (for a given spacing sequence g = (g\_1, …, g\_{n−1}), g\_k ≥ m\_F): X\_1 = Y\_1 = 1; X\_{k+1} = d\_x,i^(g\_k+1) X\_k + 1, Y\_{k+1} = d\_y,j^(g\_k+1) Y\_k + 1; at the k-th pairing beat w\_i ← min(hi\_ij, max(lo\_ij, w\_i + A₊X\_k − A₋Y\_k)), starting from w\_i = b\_ij. w\_i(p\_n + 1; g) is the value after n pairings, and Φ(g) := Σ\_{i∈A} w\_i(p\_n + 1; g). During the formation period only pairing beats have entry and target firing, and the other units do not affect the target column; hence Φ(g) is computed from the declared coefficients and g alone, without running the other units and without adjudicating any duty.  
　Δ̄\_F := A₊/(1 − d\_x,i^(m\_F+1)) − A₋, Δ̄\_F⁺ := max(0, Δ̄\_F) (when d\_x,i \< 1; used in the no-clipping antecedents of Corollary 14-1′ and Proposition 14-2).  
4.5  Source Conditions at the First Pairing Beat  
Write σ₁ = (a₁, I) for the letter of the first pairing beat p\_1.  
　F3: (α) Σ\_{i∈pat(a₁)} b\_ij \< θ\_j, or (β) for every k ≠ j, Σ\_{i∈pat(a₁)} b\_ik ≥ 0. When the entry pattern of the first pairing letter is exactly A, (α) follows from the second clause of K3; when all baselines are nonnegative, (β) always holds.  
　F3*: there is a declared letter a′ with pat(a′) ≤ pat(a₁) (position-wise) such that (i) pat(a′) is disjoint from A, or Σ\_{i∈pat(a′)} b\_ij \< θ\_j; and (ii) for every k ≠ j, D\_k(a′) := Σ\_{i∈pat(a₁)∖pat(a′)} b\_ik ≥ 0. (α) of F3 is a′ = a₁ and (β) is a′ = none, so F3 ⇒ F3*.  
　F3°: for every k ≠ j, θ\_k \> 0 and v\_reset,k \< θ\_k. It constrains only the declared parameters of the other units, not their input or state; it is not “the other units are at rest.”  
　F3ᵤ := F3 ∨ F3\* ∨ F3° (F3 is already contained in F3\*; its name is kept to mark the most common special case).

### 4.6  Readings and Independence

　Vocabulary independence: every item of C\_LS⁺ mentions only the declared law coefficients (λ, θ, v\_reset, d\_x, d\_y, A₊, A₋, ρ, b, lo, hi), the resolution ε, the alphabet patterns, properties of the letter sequence (pairing, rest, probe, spacings m, m\_F, count n) and the initial state; it contains no TraceSet, Part, HDyn, q, r, Cond, Act, HistCond, Org(T), Fld, Q\_F or any relabelling of them. “T” is a set of synapses specified by (A, j), not an assertion of a duty.  
　Verification independence: every item can be verified without running the dynamics, without identifying which processes actually bear the duties within W, and without adjudicating any duty (check\_C, check\_regime and f3\_mode in Appendix A.1, and f3star, f3deg, certified\_probes, formation\_outcome and f2prime in A.2, never call step). The Φ form is not a closed form, but it is only a rational recursion for the target column, so the two closed-form sufficient versions F2 and F2′ are kept as well.  
　Parameter region: K2′ contains a product of coefficients, K3 a power of λ, and F0 and F2 rational expressions, minima and the integers n, m\_F; when the parameters vary jointly, the region is in general not a polyhedron. Once λ, θ, d\_x, d\_y, m, m\_F, n and the alphabet are fixed, the slice in (A₊, A₋, b, hi, ε) is given by finitely many linear inequalities, some of them strict: it is a rational polyhedral region that may have open faces (the “Σ min ≥ θ” of F2(a) is equivalent to the sum being ≥ θ for every term-by-term choice). When Φ(g) replaces F2, Φ is a piecewise rational function of (A₊, A₋, b, hi), the corresponding slice is in general not a polyhedron, and it is described only as a region given by explicit rational conditions.  
　Quantifiers: the host family 𝒦\_LS(j, A); the driving-regime class is the set of all letter sequences satisfying F1 ∧ R1 (after the formation period, length and content arbitrary, input to the other units arbitrary); W ranges over all windows satisfying R2⁺; T is fixed once for all; for each trajectory a witness beat exists; under deterministic dynamics the conclusion is universal over paths.

5\. Laws of Dynamics

### 5.1  The Laws

The laws are the program text of Definitions 1–3 of Chapter 11, with the source of each item given in the mapping table of 4.2; C\_LS⁺ introduces no new law. The following lemmas hold for every legitimate trajectory and use only Definitions 2 and 3.

### 5.2  Dynamics Lemmas

Lemma 14.1 (bounds on membrane potential and eligibility traces). Assume S1, S2, R1. Starting from I0, for every beat t: v\_j(t) ∈ \[0, θ\_j), x\_i(t) ≥ 0, y\_j(t) ≥ 0, w(t) ∈ \[lo, hi\], vt\_j(t) ≥ Σ\_{i: s\_i(t)=1} w\_ij(t) ≥ 0; when d\_y,j \< 1, also y\_j(t) ≤ y\_sup.  
Proof: induction on t. vt\_j = λ\_j v\_j + Σ\_i w\_ij s\_i + I\_j, and every term is nonnegative (λ\_j ≥ 0, v\_j ≥ 0, w ≥ lo ≥ 0, I\_j ≥ 0), so vt\_j ≥ Σ\_{s\_i=1} w\_ij ≥ 0. If g\_j = 1, v′\_j = v\_reset,j = 0; otherwise v′\_j = vt\_j ∈ \[0, θ\_j). y′\_j = d\_y y\_j + g\_j ≥ 0, and when d\_y \< 1 it is ≤ d\_y·y\_sup + 1 = y\_sup. x′\_i = d\_x x\_i + s\_i ≥ 0. Clipping puts w′ in \[lo, hi\]. ∎  
Lemma 14.2 (decay under rest). If beats t − m, …, t − 1 are all rest beats for j, then λ\_j v\_j(t) \< λ\_j^(m+1)·θ\_j when λ\_j \> 0, and λ\_j v\_j(t) = 0 when λ\_j = 0.  
Proof: on a rest beat vt\_j = λ\_j v\_j \< θ\_j (Lemma 14.1, λ\_j ≤ 1), there is no firing, and v′\_j = λ\_j v\_j. Hence v\_j(t) = λ\_j^m v\_j(t − m), while v\_j(t − m) \< θ\_j. ∎  
Lemma 14.3 (formation accumulation). Assume I0, F1, S1–S3, P0, K1. Then (1) at beat p\_1 the component of PLUS\_ij (i ∈ A) is A₊ \> ε and the pre-beat z\_ij = 0, so z\_ij has at p\_1 a formation record by component-wise threshold crossing (D16 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*); (2) if F0, then for i ∈ A, w\_ij(p\_n + 1) ≥ ω′\_i ≥ ω\_i; (3) if d\_x,i \< 1, then w\_ij(p\_n + 1) ≤ b\_ij + n·Δ̄\_F⁺; (4) w\_ij(p\_n + 1) = w\_i(p\_n + 1; g) (F0 not needed; when the formation-amount condition is taken in form (iii), the Σ\_{i∈A} w\_ij(p\_n + 1) ≥ θ\_j and z \> ε required by Lemmas 14.4 and 14.6 follow directly from this).  
Proof: on the rest beats before p\_n there is no firing (the argument of Lemma 14.2) and no entry, and ρ = 0, so no event acts on w\_ij and w\_ij is unchanged; x and y decay by d\_x and d\_y. On a pairing beat vt\_j ≥ I\_j ≥ θ\_j, so the unit fires; PLUS\_ij = A₊x′\_i with x′\_i = d\_x x\_i + 1 ≥ 1; MINUS\_ij = −A₋y′\_j. The x′\_i and y′\_j of the k-th pairing beat are exactly X\_k and Y\_k of 4.4, and clipping is as in the recursion of 4.4, which is (4). Assume F0: from Y\_1 = 1 and Y\_{k+1} ≤ ρ\_F·Y\_k + 1, induction gives Y\_k ≤ Ȳ\_k ≤ Ȳ\_F. Hence the pre-clip increment is ≥ A₊ − A₋Ȳ\_k ≥ δ\_F \> 0, there is no lower clipping, and w′ ≥ min(hi, w + A₊ − A₋Ȳ\_k); induction on k gives (2). (1): x\_i(p\_1) = 0 and x′\_i = 1. (3): x′\_i ≤ 1/(1 − d\_x,i^(m\_F+1)) and y′ ≥ 1, so the pre-clip increment is ≤ Δ̄\_F and w′ ≤ max(w, w + Δ̄\_F). ∎  
Lemma 14.3′ (target-column facts at the first pairing beat). Assume I0, F1, S1–S3, P0. Then in the pre-beat state of p\_1: (1) every Structural Position has w\_ik = b\_ik (every k, other columns included); (2) x = 0; (3) v\_j = 0 and y\_j = 0. The v\_k and y\_k of the other units (k ≠ j) may be nonzero, and the whole-network resting condition P4R does not hold in general.  
Proof: before p\_1 the entry is all 0 at every beat (F1), so x stays 0, and there is no SYN and no MINUS; PLUS\_ik occurs only when g\_k = 1, with component A₊x′\_i = 0; ρ = 0 gives no RELAX; the pre-clip sum equals w, so there is no CLIP; hence w is unchanged. For the target j: I\_j = 0 and there is no entry, so vt\_j = λ\_j v\_j, which stays 0 starting from 0; there is no firing, and y\_j stays 0. ∎  
Lemma 14.4 (self-sustainment invariant). Assume S1–S4, P0, K2′, R1. If at beat t (t \> p\_n) Σ\_{i∈A} w\_ij(t) ≥ θ\_j, then w\_ij(t + 1) ≥ w\_ij(t) for every i ∈ A. Together with Lemma 14.3 and the formation-amount condition: w\_ij (i ∈ A) is nondecreasing on \[p\_n + 1, ∞), and Σ\_{i∈A} w\_ij ≥ θ\_j holds throughout.  
Proof: two kinds of letter. (i) The letter contains A: by S4 all entries of A are active together; vt\_j ≥ Σ\_{i∈A} w\_ij ≥ θ\_j (Lemma 14.1), so j fires; for i ∈ A, PLUS\_ij = A₊x′\_i ≥ A₊. If A₋ = 0, the MINUS component is 0 and the pre-clip increment is ≥ A₊ ≥ 0 (d\_y \< 1 not needed); otherwise d\_y \< 1, MINUS\_ij = −A₋y′\_j ≥ −A₋·y\_sup, ρ = 0, and the pre-clip increment is ≥ A₊ − A₋y\_sup ≥ 0 (K2′). (ii) The letter does not contain A: MINUS\_ij does not occur; PLUS\_ij ≥ 0 (if j fires); ρ = 0; the pre-clip sum is ≥ w. In both cases the pre-clip sum is ≥ w ≥ lo, and w′ = min(hi, pre-clip sum) ≥ w. Induction gives the second part. ∎  
Reading: through its own action on the gate, the Trace-Response makes every beat with A as input fire, so that the depression component is always held below the potentiation component; no subsequent input can make it decrease. This is maintenance “issuing from the Trace-Responses and acting on the Trace-Responses,” one concrete realization of “remaining unchanged because effective actions cancel is maintenance” in Section 3 of *The General Form of Field Concretization*.  
Lemma 14.5 is stated in terms of certificate probes and is placed in 5.3.  
Lemma 14.6 (availability and retention). For t \> p\_n, every position of T is available (D17 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*: z \> ε and a formation record earlier than t); at window end z\_ij(t\_e + 1) \> ε.  
Proof: the formation record is at p\_1 \< t (Lemma 14.3(1)); z\_ij(t) ≥ w\_ij(p\_n + 1) − b\_ij \> ε (Lemmas 14.3 and 14.4 and clause (b) of the formation-amount condition). ∎  
5.3  Membrane-Potential Upper Bound Computed from the Letter Sequence Alone, and Certificate Probes  
Lemma 14.7 (upper bound with a strictness flag). Assume □C\_hold and C\_init. For t \> p\_n define recursively a pair (U(t), s(t)), with s ∈ {strict, non-strict}:  
　U(p\_n + 1) := 0, s := non-strict;  
　if the letter of beat t contains A: (U, s)(t + 1) := (0, non-strict);  
　otherwise let B := λ\_j U(t) + Σ\_{i: s\_i(t)=1} hi\_ij + I\_j(t): if B ≥ θ\_j, (U, s)(t + 1) := (θ\_j, strict); if B \< θ\_j, (U, s)(t + 1) := (B, s(t) ∧ \[λ\_j \> 0\]).  
Then for every t \> p\_n: 0 ≤ v\_j(t) \< U(t) when s(t) is strict, and 0 ≤ v\_j(t) ≤ U(t) when it is non-strict; and always v\_j(t) \< θ\_j. The recursion reads only the declared parameters (λ, θ, hi) and the letter sequence.  
Proof: induction on t. At the start and after beats containing A: v\_j = 0 = U, and the non-strict bound holds (p\_n and beats containing A fire and reset, Lemmas 14.3 and 14.4). Other beats: (i) if the unit fires at that beat, v′ = 0; when B ≥ θ\_j, 0 \< θ\_j = U′; when B \< θ\_j and strictness is required, s(t) is strict and λ\_j \> 0, so U(t) \> v\_j(t) ≥ 0 and B ≥ λ\_j U(t) \> 0 = v′. (ii) If it does not fire, v′ = vt\_j = λ\_j v\_j + Σ w\_ij s\_i + I\_j \< θ\_j. Shift: Σ w\_ij s\_i + I\_j ≤ Σ hi\_ij s\_i + I\_j, which does not affect strictness; scaling by a positive coefficient: when λ\_j \> 0 and v\_j \< U, λ\_j v\_j \< λ\_j U, so strictness is preserved; when λ\_j = 0, λ\_j v\_j = λ\_j U = 0, which gives only the non-strict bound. Hence when B \< θ\_j the strictness of v′ relative to B is s(t) ∧ \[λ\_j \> 0\]. Capping: when B ≥ θ\_j, U′ = θ\_j is taken, and v′ \< θ\_j always holds, so the bound is strict. ∎  
Definition (certificate probe). A probe beat t\* in W (t\* \> p\_n) is called a certificate probe if  
　(a) s(t*) is strict, λ\_j \> 0 and λ\_j U(t*) + Σ\_{i∈A} b\_ij ≤ θ\_j, or  
　(b) λ\_j U(t\*) + Σ\_{i∈A} b\_ij \< θ\_j.  
It is decided from the declared parameters and the letter sequence alone.  
Note 1 (a test without the strictness flag is insufficient): if only the numerical value of U is used, with the test “λU + Σb \< θ, or U = θ, λ \> 0 and λθ + Σb ≤ θ,” then when K3 holds with equality and U shrinks under rest from θ to λ^m θ, the information about the strict endpoint is lost and clean probes are missed. Example: λ\_j = 1/2, W0 = 7/16 (Σ\_A b = 7/8, λ³θ + Σb = 1, K3(2) with equality), formation followed by (B, I₀ = 10), two rest beats and an A probe: (U, s) is successively (1, strict), (1/2, strict), (1/4, strict), λU + Σb = 1; the value-only test is empty, the certificate probes are \[21\], and after T is actually set to b the probe input is 7/8 \< 1 (Appendix B.9 E1).  
Note 2 ((b) must not be mechanically changed to “≤”): when the upper bound can be attained (s non-strict), that would issue false certificates. Example: λ\_j = 1/2, Σ\_A b = 3/4, one beat after formation that only injects I₀ = 1/2 and does not fire, immediately followed by a probe: (U, s) = (1/2, non-strict), and the upper bound is attained exactly; “λU + Σb ≤ θ” is mechanically true, while after T is actually set to b the gating argument is 1 = θ and the gate does not close; the set of certificate probes is empty (the trap example of Appendix B.9 E3).  
Lemma 14.7′ (inclusion: m-clean probes are certificate probes). Assume K3(m) holds, t\* is an m-clean probe and t\* \> p\_n. Then t\* is a certificate probe.  
Proof: write t₀ := max(t\* − m, p\_n + 1), (U₀, s₀) := (U(t₀), s(t₀)), m′ := t\* − t₀ ≤ m. The beats from t₀ to t\* − 1 are rest beats for j (if t₀ = p\_n + 1 \> t\* − m, then U₀ = 0), and on them B = λ\_j U (no entry, I\_j = 0).  
　λ\_j = 0: when m′ ≥ 1 the rest beats give U = 0; when m′ = 0, λ\_j U = 0. In either case λ\_j U(t\*) + Σb = Σb \< θ\_j (second clause of K3), which is (b).  
　λ\_j \> 0, U₀ \< θ\_j: on every rest beat B = λ\_j U ≤ U \< θ\_j, so there is no capping, and U(t*) = λ\_j^(m′) U₀. If m′ = m, λ\_j U(t*) = λ\_j^(m+1) U₀ \< λ\_j^(m+1) θ\_j; if m′ \< m, then t₀ = p\_n + 1, U₀ = 0 and λ\_j U(t*) = 0 \< λ\_j^(m+1) θ\_j. Hence λ\_j U(t*) + Σb \< λ\_j^(m+1)θ\_j + Σb ≤ θ\_j, which is (b)—whether or not s₀ is strict. In particular, in the branch where s₀ is non-strict and U₀ \< θ\_j: when λ\_j \> 0 a non-strict positive upper bound can indeed occur and does not come only from reset (U = 0)—for example, after formation, injecting only I\_j = 1/2 from the reset state (λ\_j = 1/2, θ\_j = 1) without firing gives (U, s) = (1/2, non-strict), with the upper bound attained exactly; after m rest beats, λ\_j U ≤ λ\_j^(m+1)U₀ \< λ\_j^(m+1)θ\_j (λ\_j \> 0), and together with K3(m) this gives λ\_j U(t\*) + Σb \< θ\_j, strictly subthreshold, which is (b).  
　λ\_j \> 0, U₀ = θ\_j: the value θ\_j of the upper bound comes only from capping (the branch B \< θ\_j gives U \< θ\_j, and reset gives U = 0 \< θ\_j), and the recursion has already made s₀ strict, so this branch is the strict case. When λ\_j \< 1, after m′ beats U = λ\_j^(m′) θ\_j, strict (at the first rest beat B = λ\_j θ\_j \< θ\_j, and strictness is preserved), and m′ = m (U₀ ≠ 0), so λ\_j U + Σb = λ\_j^(m+1)θ\_j + Σb ≤ θ\_j, which is (a); when λ\_j = 1, B = θ\_j and each beat is capped to (θ\_j, strict), K3 reduces to Σb = 0, and λ\_j U + Σb = θ\_j ≤ θ\_j, which is (a). ∎  
Corollary: R2 ⊆ R2⁺; a theorem stated with clean probes is a special case of Theorem 14-1⁺; a probe beat immediately following a letter containing A is a certificate probe (U = 0, by the second clause of K3).  
Lemma 14.5 (threshold-gating pivot at a certificate probe). Assume C\_LS⁺, and let t\* be a certificate probe beat in W. Then (1) g\_j(t*) = 1; (2) let σ be the 𝒟₁ setting that at u = t* sets every position of A\_T(t*) to b (Definition D-1 of Chapter 11; by Lemma 14.6, A\_T(t*) = T); then vt\_j^σ(t*) = λ\_j v\_j(t*) + Σ\_{i∈A} b\_ij \< θ\_j, g\_j^σ(t*) = 0, and PLUS\_ij@t* does not occur under σ; (3) the actual PLUS\_ij@t\* has component A₊x′\_i ≥ A₊ \> ε; (4) the actual SYN\_ij@t\* has component w\_ij(t\*) ≠ b\_ij.  
Proof: (1) By Lemmas 14.1 and 14.4: vt\_j ≥ Σ\_{i∈A} w\_ij ≥ θ\_j. (2) At a probe beat the entry is exactly A and I\_j = 0. Case (a): s is strict and λ\_j \> 0, so λ\_j v\_j \< λ\_j U, hence vt\_j^σ \< λ\_j U + Σb ≤ θ\_j; case (b): λ\_j v\_j ≤ λ\_j U, hence vt\_j^σ ≤ λ\_j U + Σb \< θ\_j. The occurrence condition of PLUS contains g\_j (Definition 2). (3) x′\_i = d\_x x\_i + 1 ≥ 1, and K1. (4) By Lemma 14.6, z\_ij(t\*) \> ε. ∎  
Note: the second clause of K3 cannot be omitted. With only the first clause, (2) fails on members with λ\_j = 0 and Σ\_{i∈A} b\_ij = θ\_j: in the minimal example λ = 0, θ = 2, A = {entry 1}, b₁ = 2, after T is set to b at the probe beat vt = 2 ≥ θ and the gate stays open; Theorem D2 of Chapter 11 gives HDyn = 0 when B\_FD is declared (Appendix B.2 S3b, B.3 X5). The reason is that the strict inequality comes from λ\_j v\_j \< λ\_j^(m+1)θ\_j; when λ\_j = 0 the product is 0 and strictness is lost.

6\. Theorem Group

### 6.1  Main Theorem

Theorem 14-1⁺ (self-sustainment–pivot theorem for the event-gating class). Let G ∈ 𝒦\_g, with j, A, n, m\_F, m declared, and let C\_LS⁺ hold (the full table of 4.3, including one of the formation-amount conditions and F3ᵤ). Then for every letter sequence h satisfying F1 and R1 (after the formation period the number of beats, the letters and the nonnegative teaching current are arbitrary, and input to the other units is arbitrary) and every window W satisfying R2⁺, on the declared instance χ = χ(G, h, W) (algorithmic level, model mode) and T = {(i, j) : i ∈ A}:  
　(1) Dynamics facts: the conclusions of Lemmas 14.1–14.7 hold;  
　(2) TraceSet(χ, T), Part(χ, T) and HDyn(χ, T) hold simultaneously, and hence Fld(χ);  
　(3) Antecedents consumed: the items listed in 7.2 of Chapter 11 for positive determinations of TraceSet, Part and HDyn and of Fld (E1–E8, E12, E14, E15, E25, E26, E21 and Theorem COR); E10 (B\_FD), E13 (EF1′), E22 (N′) and E27 (B\_OD) are not consumed; if some certificate probe t\* in W satisfies NS*(t*), E11 is not consumed, and otherwise H\_IND (E11) is carried in addition;  
　(4) Quantifiers: universal over h and W; T is the same for all h and W; the witness event PLUS\_ij@t\* is obtained trajectory by trajectory; the dynamics is deterministic, and the conclusion is universal over paths.  
Status: Conditional on C\_LS⁺ and the antecedents listed above; ¬C does not imply ¬Fld.  
Proof (dynamics facts and read-back kept apart): the dynamics facts follow from Lemmas 14.1–14.7. Read-back:  
　(a) r: by Lemma 14.3′ and Proposition 14-5(1), under F3ᵤ the effective write PLUS\_ij acting on T at the first pairing beat p\_1 (Lemma 14.3(1)) is changed by some admissible same-beat removal satisfying the no-opening condition, i.e. PS1 and M1₀ of criterion PS hold (4.3 of Chapter 11); the removal used and the no-opening condition are both checked directly at the first pairing beat, without assuming that the other units are at rest; by Theorem COR(1), this write has a signal source under the declared source reading, and no \[correspondence\] antecedent is consumed (those consumed are E1, E2, E4, E5, E8, E14, E26 and E21); hence r\_R(T) = 1. If it is known in addition that the pre-beat state of p\_1 satisfies the whole-network P4R, Theorem SUB gives the same conclusion; P4R is not an antecedent of this theorem.  
　(b) q: by Lemma 14.6 and the q of 3.4 of Chapter 11 (z \> ε at window end and a formation record), q(T) = 1, consuming E5, E6, E8.  
　(c) TraceSet = q ∧ r ∧ Ident ∧ HistEv: Ident by POS (E7), HistEv by REC (E5).  
　(d) HDyn: take e = PLUS\_ij@t\* (i ∈ A), with tgt(e) ∈ T. a(e) = 1 (Lemma 14.5(3), Lemma 3.1 of Chapter 11). FD\_𝒟₁(e) = 1, with implemented witness (u = t*, σ) (Lemma 14.5(2)) and set of changed positions Q = T; Path\_act(Q × {t*}, e) = 1, because the read set of PLUS contains the gating read set, and at the probe beat the gating read set contains every position of T (Definition 5 of Chapter 11; in r\_layer\_witness.py of Appendix A.5 the reads of PLUS are gr ∪ {x\_i}); by Lemma 3.2, e satisfies HistCond; by Theorem D2(i), HDyn(χ, T) = 1 at the object level. Clipping: if (i, j) is not clipped at t*, e is also counted under the joint reading; if it is clipped but hi\_ij − w\_ij(t*) \> ε, the net increment of the joint event is hi − w \> ε and becomes ≤ 0 under σ (PLUS does not occur), so the joint reading also gives 1; the two readings agree, and E11 is not consumed. When NS\* fails, only the separate reading gives 1, and E11 is carried (Proposition 14-4).  
　(e) Part: the carrier of e is in B and e satisfies HistCond, so the lemma of Section 4 of *The General Form of Field Concretization* (historical conditioning implies organizational participation) and the Convergence Round give Cond, and hence Part = 1; alternatively from κ = 1 for SYN\_ij@t\* (Lemma 14.5(4) and the isomorphic counterpart of Lemma 3.2).  
　(f) Fld: by Theorem R7(1) and Corollary 1 of *The General Form of Field Concretization*. ∎  
Corollary 14-1′ (a regime subclass that does not consume E11). If in addition some i ∈ A has d\_x,i \< 1, every beat between p\_n and t\* is a rest beat for j, and b\_ij + n·Δ̄\_F⁺ + A₊·x\_sup,i ≤ hi\_ij, then (i, j) is not clipped at t*, NS*(t\*) holds, and Theorem 14-1⁺ does not consume E11.  
Proof: w\_ij(t\*) = w\_ij(p\_n + 1) ≤ b\_ij + n·Δ̄\_F⁺ (Lemma 14.3(3)), and the pre-clip sum is ≤ w + A₊x′\_i ≤ w + A₊x\_sup,i ≤ hi\_ij. ∎  
Corollary 14-1″ (in-boundary self-feedback type). Under the open-loop declaration (E23 of Chapter 11), Theorem B2 makes the in-boundary self-feedback type agree with HDyn event by event; the carrier of e and the route from T through the gate to e both lie in B, so under the conclusion of Theorem 14-1⁺ the in-boundary self-feedback type = 1. It is a corollary, not a separate independent output.  
6.2  Two Readings and E11: Proposition 14-4  
Proposition 14-4 (two sufficient ranges). Under C\_LS⁺:  
　(1) If some certificate probe t\* in W satisfies NS\*, then at that beat both the separate and the joint reading give an effective action on T and an HDyn witness, and E11 is not consumed.  
　(2) If at the window start t\_s every i ∈ A has hi\_ij − w\_ij(t\_s) ≤ ε, then under the joint reading every joint net increment at the T positions within W is ≤ ε, there is no effective joint action on T, the joint-reading HDyn of Theorem D2 of Chapter 11 does not give 1 while the separate reading gives 1, and the verdict is ⊥\_Ind; here E11 cannot be omitted.  
Proof: (1) As in the clipping clause of part (d) of the proof of Theorem 14-1⁺. (2) By Lemma 14.4, within W w\_ij is nondecreasing and ≤ hi, so the net increment w\_ij(t + 1) − w\_ij(t) of each beat is ≥ 0 and their sum is ≤ hi\_ij − w\_ij(t\_s) ≤ ε; joint events take the net increment as their component, and none of them is effective. ∎  
Reading: after saturation T is held at its upper bound by “the potentiation component cancelling the clipping component.” Under the separate reading this is maintenance, unchanged because effective actions cancel; under the joint reading it is an indecomposable joint process with zero net change, whose criterion of action is exactly the unanswered question listed in Section 8 of *The General Form of Field Concretization*, also listed as U6 in Chapter 11. Whether E11 stays or goes therefore depends on that unanswered question. The two sufficient ranges do not exhaust all windows—for example, the window start is unsaturated but saturation occurs before the first certificate probe, or NS\* fails at every certificate probe while some other beat carries an effective pivot action under the joint reading—and the complete characterization of this intermediate range is open (U14-14).  
6.3  The Source at the First Pairing Beat: Proposition 14-5  
Proposition 14-5. Under I0, F1, S1–S3, P0, K1:  
　(1) If F3\* or F3° holds, then for any prefix satisfying F1 (with arbitrary input to the other units), at the first pairing beat p\_1 there is an admissible same-beat removal that satisfies the no-opening condition and changes some effective write acting on T (M1₀).  
　(2) Assume in addition S4 (the alphabet is A-indecomposable). If F3ᵤ fails and there is a blocking unit k ≠ j with λ\_k v\_reset,k ≥ θ\_k, λ\_k v\_reset,k + Σ\_{i∈pat(a₁)} b\_ik \< θ\_k, and λ\_k v\_reset,k + Σ\_{i∈pat(a′)} b\_ik ≥ θ\_k for every candidate a′ satisfying F3\*(i), then there is a prefix satisfying F1 such that at p\_1 no admissible same-beat removal satisfies M1₀.  
Proof: (1) Before beat p\_1 the target column has w = b, x = 0, v\_j = y\_j = 0, and w = b in every column (Lemma 14.3′). F3\*: take the removal “entry changed to a′, I\_j removed to 0, other injections kept.” If pat(a′) is disjoint from A, x′\_i = 0 (i ∈ A), and the component of PLUS\_ij changes from A₊ to 0 or PLUS\_ij does not occur; otherwise vt′\_j = Σ\_{pat(a′)} b\_ij \< θ\_j, gate j closes, and PLUS\_ij does not occur. For k ≠ j, vt′\_k − vt\_k = −D\_k(a′) ≤ 0: no opening. F3°: take the removal “entry changed to none, all injections removed to 0.” vt′\_j = 0 and gate j closes; for k ≠ j, vt′\_k = λ\_k v\_k; v\_reset,k \< θ\_k gives v\_k \< θ\_k (without firing v′ = vt \< θ, with firing v′ = v\_reset \< θ), so λ\_k v\_k ≤ v\_k \< θ\_k when v\_k ≥ 0, and λ\_k v\_k ≤ 0 \< θ\_k when v\_k \< 0; hence g′\_k = 0: no opening. Both removals belong to the removal domain of 3.3 of Chapter 11 (the entry takes a declared letter position-wise no greater than the actual pattern, and each component of the teaching current takes its original value or 0).  
　(2) Prefix: the beat before p\_1 has letter none and injects only into k, with I\_k ≥ θ\_k, so that k fires and v\_k = v\_reset,k; at p\_1, I\_k = 0. By S4, the entry pattern of an admissible removal is all 1 or all 0 on A, so a removal that changes an effective write on T must close gate j or remove the whole of A (PLUS\_ij depends only on g\_j and x′\_i, MINUS\_ij only on s\_i and g\_j; at p\_1, x = y\_j = 0); that is, the entry must take some a′ satisfying F3\*(i) (if A is kept and I\_j is not removed, vt\_j ≥ I\_j ≥ θ\_j and the gate does not close). In the actual run, vt\_k = λ\_k v\_reset,k + Σ\_{pat(a₁)} b\_ik \< θ\_k and k does not fire; after the removal vt′\_k = λ\_k v\_reset,k + Σ\_{pat(a′)} b\_ik ≥ θ\_k (the removal only takes each injection at its original value 0 or 0), so k newly opens, violating the no-opening condition. ∎  
Reading: F3° is the exact antecedent needed by the removal “remove down to the rest letter”—what is needed is only a relation between the thresholds and reset values of the other units, not rest of the whole network, and the input regime is not narrowed. The narrower single blocking condition (v\_reset,k = 0) cannot be satisfied when F3° holds, since the candidate none is always an admissible removal; blocking must therefore require λ\_k v\_reset,k ≥ θ\_k. The antecedent S4 of (2) cannot be omitted: when the alphabet contains a letter that takes only part of A, removing to that letter can cancel the PLUS\_ij of the removed entries while gate j stays open, satisfying M1₀ without involving the closing of gate j. Example: A = {entry 0, entry 1}, letters none, A, partial = (1, 0), λ = θ = (1, 1), v\_reset = (0, 2), d\_x = d\_y = 0, A₊ = 1/2, A₋ = ρ = 0, baselines b = \[\[1, −3\], \[1, 1\]\]; F3ᵤ fails and the arithmetic of the blocking condition holds (output 1), yet when the first pairing beat is removed to (partial, I = (1, 0)) both gates are unchanged while PLUS\_10 changes from 1/2 to 0; and for every permitted first-pairing prefix (pre-beat v\_1 of output 1 in \[0, 1) or equal to the reset value 2) this removal causes no new opening. With partial removed (S4 holds), the same parameters give 0 admissible same-beat removals satisfying M1₀ (Appendix B.10 G2). Hence the blocking test blocker2 of Appendix A.2 is used only on alphabets satisfying S4 (the alphabets of the random members of Appendix B.8 D3 all satisfy S4); for decomposable alphabets a separate blocking criterion that exhausts the candidates “changing only part of the A writes” is needed, and has not been given (U14-8′). The antecedents of the main theorem 14-1⁺ include S4, so this case does not arise there.  
Remaining (U14-8′): the region where neither F3ᵤ holds nor a blocking unit exists; in the blocking case, whether r can be obtained from removals at later pairing beats, cross-beat removal M1\_aux (carrying EF1′) or M2 (carrying B\_OD). A witness not obtained is recorded as ⊥, not 0; under the declared source reading the complete Mark^A is not exhausted by the criteria (4.3 of Chapter 11).  
6.4  Tightness, Formation Threshold and Same-Family Equivalence Characterization  
Proposition 14-2 (K3 cannot be relaxed). Assume all items of C\_LS⁺ other than K3(m) hold, the formation-amount condition is taken in form (i), and K3(m) fails; assume in addition that for every i ∈ A, d\_x,i \< 1 and b\_ij + n·Δ̄\_F⁺ + A₊x\_sup,i ≤ hi\_ij (no clipping at the probe beat). Then there are a history h satisfying F1 and R1 and a window W containing an m-clean probe such that every effective event acting on T within W has FD\_𝒟₁ = 0 while Path\_slot = 1; hence Theorem D2 of Chapter 11 gives HDyn(T) = 0\[B\_FD\] (⊥\_Over when B\_FD is not declared), that is, under B\_FD, R(T) fails on this instance. The endpoint λ\_j = 1 is included.  
Construction: (i) Σ\_{i∈A} b\_ij ≥ θ\_j: h = formation period + m rest beats + probe beat + rest; W = \[t*, t* + k\]. (ii) Σ b \< θ, λ\_j \> 0 and λ\_j^(m+1)θ\_j + Σ b \> θ\_j: let v\* := (θ\_j − Σ b)/λ\_j^(m+1) \< θ\_j and take c ∈ \[v*, θ\_j); h = formation period + rest + preload beat (entry all 0, I\_j = c) + m rest beats + probe beat + rest; W as above. When λ\_j = 1, K3 reduces to Σb = 0; if Σb \> 0, take v* = θ − Σb; rest causes no decay, and the construction goes through unchanged.  
Proof: at the end of the formation period v\_j = 0 (the last pairing fires and resets, followed by rest); at the preload beat vt = c \< θ, there is no firing, v′ = c, and with no entry and no firing nothing acts on w. For any u ≤ t\* and any setting σ in 𝒟₁(u) (each position taking a value in \[b, hi\]): the trajectories of v, x, y are unchanged—w affects vt only through beats with entry, and before t\* the only beats with entry are pairing beats, on which vt ≥ I\_j ≥ θ always fires and resets; x depends only on the letters; y depends only on the gate. w^σ(t*) ≥ b. Hence vt\_j^σ(t*) ≥ λ\_j v\_j(t*) + Σ b ≥ θ, and the gate is open under every setting. At t* the actual events acting on T are only PLUS and MINUS (by the no-clipping antecedent), whose occurrence and components depend only on x′, y′, g, s and are unchanged under every setting; the other beats in W are rest beats, with no events on T. Path\_slot = 1 (the gate reads T). Hence the case falls into tier (b) (4.2 of Chapter 11). ∎  
Reading: K3 is an exact threshold on the declared regime class; the necessity of its second clause is given by the λ = 0 example in the Note to Lemma 14.5 in 5.3. Members with clipping at the probe beat are not within the antecedents: a clipping event reads the w at its own position and can bear HDyn separately, a trivial route of the same type as shallowing, which this proposition does not discuss.  
Proposition 14-3 (K2′ is equivalent to self-sustainment). Assume d\_x,i \< 1 (i ∈ A) and the items of C\_LS⁺ other than K2′. Then K2′ holds if and only if, for every history satisfying F1 and R1, w\_ij (i ∈ A) is nondecreasing on \[p\_n + 1, ∞). When K2′ fails, there are a history satisfying F1 and R1 and a window satisfying R2 such that z\_ij ≤ ε at window end (for every i ∈ A), q(T) = 0, and R(T) fails at the object level (q is a window-end state fact and does not consume B\_FD).  
Proof: “If” is Lemma 14.4. “Only if”: K2′ fails means A₋ \> 0 and (d\_y = 1 or A₊(1 − d\_y) \< A₋). After formation, first place one m-clean probe beat (so that R2 holds), then repeat the period \[K₂ rest beats; K₁ beats with entry all 0 and I\_j = θ\_j; 1 beat with the probe letter and I\_j = θ\_j\]. When d\_y \< 1: the rest segment does not act on w, and x\_i falls to ≤ d\_x^K₂·x\_sup; in the teaching segment every beat fires with no entry, so there is no MINUS, the sum of PLUS is ≤ A₊x\_sup d\_x^(K₂+1)/(1 − d\_x), and y\_j rises to ≥ y\_sup(1 − d\_y^K₁); the probe-letter beat fires, with PLUS ≤ A₊(1 + d\_x^(K₁+K₂+1)x\_sup) and \|MINUS\| ≥ A₋y\_sup(1 − d\_y^(K₁+1)). Clipping is monotone and 1-Lipschitz, and the increase and decrease of the probe-letter beat are booked together in one step, so after one period w ≤ max(lo, w₀ + Ψ), Ψ := A₊x\_sup d\_x^(K₂+1)/(1 − d\_x) + A₊(1 + d\_x^(K₁+K₂+1)x\_sup) − A₋y\_sup(1 − d\_y^(K₁+1)). As K₁, K₂ → ∞, Ψ → A₊ − A₋y\_sup \< 0; choose K₁, K₂ with Ψ ≤ −η \< 0. When d\_y = 1, after the K₁ beats of the teaching segment y ≥ K₁ and \|MINUS\| ≥ A₋(K₁ + 1), and the same construction gives a negative increment per period for K₁ large enough. After finitely many periods w\_ij ≤ max(lo, b\_ij) ≤ b\_ij, i.e. z ≤ 0 ≤ ε; take the window end there. ∎  
Reading: the antecedent d\_x \< 1 cannot be deleted—when d\_x = 1 the eligibility trace x does not decay, potentiation grows linearly with the number of beats containing A, and the erosion construction fails (Appendix B.8 D5: A₋ = 1/12, after 200 periods min z = 7/4 and no decreasing beat). The exact condition for self-sustainment when d\_x = 1 is registered as U14-9. K2′ is necessary universally over the regime class, not for individual trajectories (the same member can still satisfy R on other trajectories).  
Proposition 14-6 (formation threshold).  
　(1) F2 ≤ F2′ ≤ Φ: for any spacing sequence g (g\_k ≥ m\_F), Σ\_{i∈A} ω\_i ≤ Σ\_{i∈A} ω′\_i ≤ Φ(g), and term by term ω\_i ≤ ω′\_i ≤ w\_i(p\_n + 1; g).  
　(2) Exactness: assume the other items of C\_LS⁺ hold (the formation-amount condition excepted), ε \> 0, and A₋ = 0 or d\_y,j \< 1. For a given g: if Φ(g) ≥ θ\_j and min\_i (w\_i(p\_n + 1; g) − b\_ij) \> ε, the conclusion of Theorem 14-1⁺ holds for every legitimate continuation; if Φ(g) \< θ\_j, there is a legitimate continuation (L rest beats followed by one probe, W = that beat) on which all events acting on T within W are ineffective, HDyn = 0 (a = 0, B\_FD not consumed), and R(T) fails.  
Proof: (1) Y\_k ≤ Ȳ\_k ≤ Ȳ\_F and X\_k ≥ 1, so each increment is ≥ A₊ − A₋Ȳ\_k ≥ δ\_F \> 0, there is no lower clipping, and w\_n ≥ min(hi, b + Σ\_k (A₊ − A₋Ȳ\_k)) ≥ min(hi, b + nδ\_F). (2) The first direction is Theorem 14-1⁺ with formation-amount condition (iii). The other direction: after formation v\_j = 0, rest does not change w, and at the probe beat vt\_j = Σ\_{i∈A} w\_i(p\_n + 1; g) \< θ\_j, so there is no firing and PLUS does not occur; the MINUS\_ij component is −A₋ d\_y y\_j(t*), with y\_j(t*) = d\_y^L y\_j(p\_n + 1) ≤ d\_y^L Ȳ\_F, so \|c\| ≤ ε for L large enough (0 when A₋ = 0); the clipping component is at most of the same magnitude; there is no RELAX. ∎  
Reading: F2 is a conservative bound; Φ is the exact threshold for the given spacing sequence (for the frozen member with equal spacing m\_F = 2, Φ first reaches ≥ 1 at n = 4, while F2′ and F2 both do so at n = 5; Appendix B.8 D4). The uniform threshold over the whole F1 class (arbitrary spacings ≥ m\_F) is inf\_g Φ(g) ≥ θ; its closed-form solution has not been completed (at each step the x and y terms vary in opposite directions with the spacing), and F2′ is a provable lower bound for it (U14-1, U14-10).  
Theorem 14-7 (same-family equivalence characterization).  
Background antecedents: S1–S4, P0, K1, F3ᵤ, I0, one fixed spacing sequence g of F1 with min\_i (w\_i(p\_n + 1; g) − b\_ij) \> ε, d\_x,i \< 1 (i ∈ A), ε \> 0; for the K3 direction, additionally the no-clipping antecedent of Proposition 14-2, with B\_FD\[𝒟₁\] declared.  
Statement: “for every legitimate continuation and every window containing an m-clean probe, R(T) holds (carrying E11 where NS\* fails)” ⇔ K2′ ∧ K3(m) ∧ Φ(g) ≥ θ\_j.  
Proof: ⇐ by Theorem 14-1⁺ (formation-amount condition (iii), Lemma 14.7′). ⇒: if K2′ fails, Proposition 14-3 gives a trajectory with q(T) = 0; if K3(m) fails, Proposition 14-2 gives a trajectory with HDyn = 0\[B\_FD\]; if Φ(g) \< θ, Proposition 14-6(2) gives a trajectory with HDyn = 0. ∎  
Reading: each item is necessary and together they are sufficient; the equivalence holds under the declared readings (B\_FD for the K3 direction, E11 at saturation) and the background antecedents, and is not an unconditional C ⇔ R. K1 is not within the equivalence characterization; it remains a background antecedent, and the conclusion of Theorem 14-7 has K1 as an antecedent. When K1 fails, whether HDyn can be borne by MINUS (whose strength depends on the gate through y′) is open; the exact role of K1 is registered as U14-11.  
6.5  Regime Extension and Stochastic Quantifiers  
　(1) Windows without a clean probe and windows with several probes: by Theorem 14-1⁺, W need only contain one certificate probe; when there are several probes, any one may be taken. A probe immediately following a letter containing A (for example, two consecutive A's with W taking the second beat) is a certificate probe.  
　(2) Still not covered: windows with A but no certificate probe (for example, when membrane potential accumulated over B beats makes the upper bound U reach its cap and λθ + Σb \> θ). There the Trace-Response may still be a pivot (the decision procedure of Chapter 11 may give HDyn = 1), but this cannot be guaranteed from the letters and the declared parameters alone; a bound on the actual w or a multi-beat functional-dependence witness is needed. Registered as U14-12.  
　(3) ρ \> 0 and A-decomposable alphabets: these do not satisfy the antecedents of Theorem 14-1⁺ (S3, S4). When ρ \> 0, relaxation reads the Trace-Response at its own position and HDyn can be borne trivially by relaxation, so the substance of the extension reduces to retention; for A-decomposable alphabets a separate balance condition between increase and decrease on single-entry letters is needed (U14-2).  
Proposition 14-8 (quantifiers under i.i.d. input). Declared reading: after the formation period the letters of the beats (entry and teaching current) are independent and identically distributed, with a nonnegative distribution π of finite support; write p\_q := π(rest for j), p\_p := π(probe letter), q := p\_q^m·p\_p \> 0.  
　(1) The probability that a window of length L contains an m-clean probe is ≥ 1 − (1 − q)^⌊L/(m+1)⌋; the probability that it contains a certificate probe is no smaller.  
　(2) On that event, the conclusion of Theorem 14-1⁺ holds universally over paths (carrying E11 where NS\* fails); hence P(R(T) holds on W) ≥ 1 − (1 − q)^⌊L/(m+1)⌋ → 1 (L → ∞).  
　(3) In an infinite continuation, m-clean probes occur infinitely often almost surely; the expected time of the first m-clean probe (counted from the end of the formation period) is ≤ (m + 1)/q.  
Proof: (1) Divide the window into ⌊L/(m+1)⌋ disjoint blocks of length m + 1; each block independently, with probability q, is “m rest beats + probe”; by Lemma 14.7′ a clean probe is a certificate probe. (2) By Theorem 14-1⁺. (3) The disjoint blocks are independent, and the second Borel–Cantelli lemma applies; the index of the first successful block is geometrically distributed with parameter q, with expectation 1/q, and each block has length m + 1. ∎  
Limits: randomness enters only the event “W contains a certificate probe”; self-sustainment, retention and the pivot are universal over paths. The probability that NS\* holds (so that E11 is not consumed) is not given; saturation can occur when there are enough beats containing A. Stochastic plasticity (the law itself random) is not within this proposition (U14-13).  
6.6  FD-Level Conditional Theorem on the Gated Extension Class  
Basis: Definition 5 (𝒦\_PA^3), Definition 7 (𝒦\_PA^≺), Proposition PA-2 (closure, unique successor, order independence) and Lemmas R1′–R3′ of 3.6 of *The Adjudication Form of Material Field Concretization*. This section uses only the class definitions and closure already proved there, not that paper's open item U13-16.  
Design 𝒦\_PA^≺⁽³⁾: gating units M (= 0), D (= 1), Z (= 2), with order M ≺ D ≺ Z, U\_MD = u (all other U = 0), gate\_of = (D, Z, Z), lo = 0, b\_iD = b\_iZ = 0. Thus the plasticity of w\_iM is gated by D; D reads the same-beat firing of M only through U; Z never fires (θ\_Z \> 0 and I\_Z = 0 in the regime), so w\_iD and w\_iZ stay 0 (potentiation is gated by Z, the depression component is −A₋y′\_Z = 0, and clipping holds them at lo = 0). T = {(i, M) : i ∈ A}.  
Condition group C\_PA (full table; only arithmetic or inspection of the letter sequence; it may not be cited by a short name detached from this table):  
　Class membership and valid parameter domain: a member of 𝒦\_PA^≺; λ, d\_x, d\_y ∈ \[0, 1\], A₊, A₋ ≥ 0, ρ = 0; U has only U\_MD = u (its support is a DAG, with order M ≺ D ≺ Z); gate\_of = (D, Z, Z); scalar valid interval \[lo, hi\] = \[0, hi\]; b\_iD = b\_iZ = 0, b\_iM ∈ \[0, hi\]; every gating unit has v\_reset = 0 and θ \> 0.  
　Initial state and protocol: resting initial state w = b, v = x = y = 0; plastic = bap = 1.  
　Formation regime: before the first pairing and between adjacent pairings, every beat is a rest beat for M and D (entry all 0, I\_M = I\_D = 0); pairing beats have entry containing A and I\_D ≥ θ\_D (arrival of the third factor); spacing ≥ m\_F, n pairings in all.  
　Subsequent regime: letters belong to the declared alphabet, injections are nonnegative, I\_Z ≡ 0; at probe beats the entry is exactly A and I\_M = I\_D = 0, preceded by m rest beats for M and D.  
　Coefficients: K1 (A₊ \> ε); K2\_D (d\_y,D \< 1 and A₊(1 − d\_y,D) ≥ A₋); K3\_M(m) (λ\_M^(m+1)θ\_M + Σ\_{i∈A} b\_iM ≤ θ\_M and Σ\_{i∈A} b\_iM \< θ\_M); PD1 (u ≥ θ\_D \> 0); θ\_Z \> 0; F0\_D (A₊ − A₋/(1 − d\_y,D^(m\_F+1)) \> 0); F2\_M (F2 of 4.4 with the δ\_F of F0\_D and b\_iM, hi, θ\_M substituted); S4.  
Theorem 14-9 (FD level). Under the full table C\_PA, for every legitimate trajectory and every window containing an m-clean probe t\*:  
　(1) after formation w\_iM (i ∈ A) is nondecreasing, and Σ\_A w\_iM ≥ θ\_M;  
　(2) at t\* both M and D fire, and PLUS\_iM is effective (≥ A₊);  
　(3) after T is set to b, M does not fire (K3\_M), the gating argument of D is λ\_D v\_D \< θ\_D (v\_D \< θ\_D, I\_D = 0, w\_iD = 0), D does not fire, and PLUS\_iM does not occur; that is, FD\_𝒟₁(PLUS\_iM@t\*) = 1;  
　(4) the actual event graph contains the path T → SYN\_iM → gate M → FIN(M, D) → gate D → PLUS\_iM;  
　(5) at window end z\_iM \> ε;  
　(6) at the first pairing beat, removal down to the rest letter (none, 0) is an admissible same-beat removal (before the first pairing M and D are at rest, v\_M = v\_D = 0, and Z does not fire, so there is no opening), and it makes PLUS\_iM not occur—an M1₀-type removal change.  
Proof: (1) On beats whose letter contains A, M fires (as in Lemma 14.1), D fires by PD1, PLUS\_iM = A₊x′\_i ≥ A₊, MINUS\_iM = −A₋y′\_D ≥ −A₋y\_sup,D, and K2\_D gives an increment ≥ 0; on beats not containing A there is no MINUS\_iM. The rest follows item by item the arguments of Lemmas 14.3–14.6, with the firing of D in place of the firing of j. ∎  
Strength: (2)–(4) together are the two inputs of Lemma 3.2 of Chapter 11 (a functional-dependence witness and an actual path), but that lemma and criterion PS are proved only for 𝒦\_g. Reading back object-level HistCond from them therefore requires “a local correspondence lemma on 𝒦\_PA^≺,” B\_η^PA, and reading back r requires “criterion PS and COR on the extension class,” B\_r^PA; neither is proved (U13-16 of Chapter 13, depending on U8 of Chapter 11). Hence the conclusion of Theorem 14-9 is R\_FD := q ∧ Act ∧ FD ∧ Path ∧ RemChange (removal change), not R; if B\_η^PA and B\_r^PA are proved, it rises to C\_PA ⇒ R under their antecedents. The gap consists specifically of: (i) the R-layer declaration of extension-class instances (the individuation of events must include FIN and the g\_M in the gating read set of D); (ii) the counterpart, under the same-beat DAG evaluation order, of “setting exclusivity and concrete rerun” in the proof of Lemma 3.2 (the order independence of PA-2(ii) is available); (iii) the extension of implementation fidelity; (iv) source chains must add U edges. None of (i)–(iv) is proved, and the corresponding R-level conclusions are not issued.  
The substantive obstacle to the fly mapping: the Trace-Response of *Drosophila* KC→MBON is dopamine-gated depression (a fact recorded on the evidence card, not yet independently verified), i.e. A₋-type components dominate. The self-sustainment of Theorem 14-9 depends on K2\_D; under a depression-dominated law K2\_D fails, and a construction of the Proposition 14-3 type shows that the Trace-Response can be eroded by subsequent input. If depression is taken as the Trace-Response (with hi − w as depth), its protection must be borne by a different mechanism (for example, MBON→DAN return, by which depression lowers DAN triggering and so reduces further gating events). The condition group for this mechanism has not been given (U14-16); the fly is not judged negative on this basis.  
6.7  The Linear Own-Position Family and the Non-Neural Side  
Lemma 14-L (linear family; lemma level). Let M(a, b, k, γ, ε) be the parameterized encoding of Proposition 5(ii) of 7.1 of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*, with b, k ≥ 0 and 0 \< γ \< 1; the history is the formation letter at beat 0 followed by an arbitrary {drive, rest} sequence; W = \[t\_s, t\_e\], 1 ≤ t\_s ≤ t\_e; T = {z1}. If (L1) a \> ε; (L2) γ·a(1 − γ)^(t\_s − 1) \> ε; (L3) a(1 − γ)^(t\_e) \> ε; (L4) the upper bound under the all-drive sequence satisfies the mechanism valid domain (Family A: z ≤ z\_max; Family E: receptor pool nonnegative and 1 + z ≤ y\_max); (L5) resolution compatibility: for every such history, every component c of the two encoded positions z1, z2 at beats 0 to t\_e satisfies c = 0 or \|c\| \> ε (component-wise compatibility, written Compat); Compat is equivalent to the following closed form L5′—γ·a(1 − γ)^(t\_e − 1) \> ε, and, when b = 0, k = 0 or k·a(1 − γ)^(t\_e − 1) \> ε, and, when b \> 0, b \> ε and (t\_e = 1 or γ·b(1 − γ)^(t\_e − 2) \> ε)—then for every such history Q{z1} = 1 in the F layer, the mechanism layer and the F layer agree beat by beat from beat 0 to t\_e + 1, and through Lemma 1 of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*, Theorem 5 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* and Corollary 1 of *The General Form of Field Concretization* this reads back as R(T) = 1 (Conditional on C1–C5, C4′ or N1–N5).  
Proof: z\_t ≥ a(1 − γ)^(t−1) (b, k ≥ 0, and drive only increases); at beat t\_s the relaxation component −γz is effective and reads an available position at its own position, giving Act ∧ HistCond\_F; Part by Supp\_act ⊆ Supp\_cond; q by L3; r by the Mark of the formation pulse; validity: the all-drive sequence is maximal beat by beat (the map is monotone), so L4 gives validity throughout; compatibility by L5 (the proof of L5′ ⇔ Compat follows).  
　L5′ ⇔ Compat: from beat 1 on, z1(t) ≥ a(1 − γ)^(t−1), with equality on the history that is all rest from beat 1 to t − 1. z2 receives no formation pulse (an unseeded position) and stays 0 before the first drive beat t₁; when b = 0, z2 stays 0, and its write k·z2 and relaxation stay 0; when b \> 0, for t \> t₁ ≥ 1, z2(t) ≥ b(1 − γ)^(t−t₁−1) ≥ b(1 − γ)^(t−2), with equality on the history whose only drive beat is beat 1, all rest afterwards. The nonzero components at beats t ≤ t\_e are only: the relaxation of z1, −γz1(t), with smallest absolute value γ·a(1 − γ)^(t\_e−1); the relaxation of z2, −γz2(t) (present when b \> 0 and t\_e ≥ 2), with smallest absolute value γ·b(1 − γ)^(t\_e−2); and the write of a drive beat, b + k·z\_i(t) (omitted when b = k = 0)—when b \> 0 it is ≥ b, with minimum b attained at the first write of z2; when b = 0 there is only the write k·z1(t) of z1, and when k \> 0 its minimum k·a(1 − γ)^(t\_e−1) is attained on the history that is all rest from beat 1 to t\_e − 1 with beat t\_e a drive beat. Every minimum is attained on one of the histories considered, so Compat holds if and only if the smallest absolute value of each kind of component that occurs is \> ε, that is, L5′. ∎  
　Checking only the formation lower bound of z1 and “b \> ε when b ≠ 0” is not enough to guarantee Compat: with a = 1, b = 0, k = 1/200, γ = 1/2, ε = 1/100, history (seed, drive) and W = \[1, 1\], the drive write 1/200 is discarded, and the final state is z1 = 1/2 in the F layer and 101/200 in the mechanism layer; with a = 1, b = 1/10, k = 0, γ = 1/10, ε = 1/100, history (seed, drive, drive) and W = \[2, 2\], the relaxation of the unseeded position z2 at the second drive beat is exactly ε and is discarded, and the final state is (1, 1/5) in the F layer and (1, 19/100) in the mechanism layer. In both examples L5′ fails, so they lie outside the antecedents of the lemma; their formal R is still 1—a fidelity failure means only that the F-layer readings do not read back, and is not a negative determination of qualification (Appendix B.10 G1, which includes the subthreshold, equality and suprathreshold branches for b = 0, k = 0 and for b = 0, k \> 0, the branches of the relaxation of the unseeded position, and agreement of L5′ with Compat on a grid of 2880 cases). Physical validity L4 is verified separately.  
Position: HDyn is borne by relaxation and has nothing to do with drive. Lemma 14-L is the beat-by-beat generalization of Propositions 1 and 4 of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* and the direct composition of Theorem 5 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* with Corollary 1 of *The General Form of Field Concretization*; its role is to supply witnesses on both sides and a contrast.  
Structural observation (only for the chosen linear own-position family): in the linear own-position family (encoding M(a, b, k, γ, ε): process components c = b + k·z read their own position, relaxation −γz), when k ≠ 0 or γ ≠ 0, Supp\_act contains the own position, so HistCond\_F holds as soon as z is available (D15, D18), HDyn depends only on the magnitude of the components, and C ⇒ R reduces to Lemma 14-L. This observation concerns only this family and does not extend to all non-neural candidates or all F-layer mechanisms.  
Routes to non-triviality: threshold gating is one route—the occurrence of the action is decided by the Trace-Response (which F does not encode) and is handled in the gating class of the R layer, which is the case of Theorem 14-1⁺. It is not the only route. D15 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* allows state-dependent bilinear support: an ungated process c = k·x·y (k ≠ 0; x ∈ T, already formed, x \> ε; y the partner position) runs every beat from the same inventory and its occurrence is not gated, while ∂c/∂x = k·y, so x is outside the functional support when y = 0 and inside it when y ≠ 0—“support can fail” does not require the occurrence of the action to change with the Trace-Response (Appendix B.9 E5: k = 1, and for y = 0, 1/2, 1, x ∈ Supp\_act is false, true, true). When k = 0, c ≡ 0, x is never in the functional support, and this state dependence does not hold. Activity in the two states is certified separately by atom; when c = 0 Act also fails, which cannot be used to deny the state-dependent change of support. Hence routes such as bilinear-partner support are not excluded; a general impossibility conclusion would first have to give a rigorous object class and prove it, and no such conclusion has been proved. The host construction for the bilinear route is registered as U14-15.  
Family 1ᵍ (threshold-scour pool-network reading; declared as a model assumption): entry i is an upstream inflow source (out-of-boundary inflow pulses); gating unit j is a sink pool (water level v, leakage coefficient λ; when the level reaches the critical value θ it scours and empties); the Structural Position w\_ij is the channel conductance from source i to sink j (the part above baseline is the Trace-Response); x\_i is the recent wetness of source i; y\_j is the loose material left after the sink's recent scour; PLUS is scour widening of the channel (according to wetness); MINUS is inflow carrying loose material into the channel and clogging it (according to loose material); I\_j is direct out-of-boundary water injection. The reading corresponds item by item to Definitions 1–2 of Chapter 11, so it belongs to 𝒦\_g; with frozen (dimensionless) constants, C\_LS⁺ holds and Theorem 14-1⁺ applies word for word at the formal layer: HDyn passes only through threshold gating, ρ = 0, and it is non-trivial.  
Physical validity: the budget of the loose-material store L at the sink is ΔL = Σ\_i PLUS\_ij − Σ\_i \|MINUS\_ij\|. C\_LS⁺ does not close this budget: on inflow beats not containing A, if there is no scour, there is only clogging and no replenishment by scour. In an adversarial history (after formation, repeat \[scour by water injection alone for 8 beats → B inflow for 3 beats without firing → rest for 6 beats\] × 60), the store falls from 219345/262144 at the end of the formation period to negative values, first at beat 95, with a minimum of −11.764318 (Appendix B.8 D8). Sufficient condition: if the initial store L₀ ≥ A₋·y\_sup·n\_in·(history length), it stays nonnegative (clogging per beat ≤ A₋·y\_sup·n\_in; 29 in the attached example); this is an additionally declared resource antecedent.  
Conclusion: the non-neural side obtains a non-trivial witness at the formal layer (the same theorem as on the neural side), but no physical construction certificate—the budget requires a resource antecedent, the constitutive relations (wetness drives widening, loose material drives clogging, critical water level) have no empirical basis, and no energy account is given. A physically non-trivial witness on the non-neural side is an open problem (U14-5: find a non-trivial non-neural mechanism whose constitutive relations are grounded and whose budget can close—the threshold-gating and bilinear-partner-support routes are equally open).  
6.8  Division of Load-Bearing and Irreducibility  
　(1) Relative to *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*: that paper gives verdicts on a declared instance; its Theorem D1 performs symbolic execution plus linear feasibility on a single instance, at a cost that grows exponentially with window length in the worst case; its 7.5 gives Fld = 1 for Instance P, and its grid of 108 combinations and 200 random histories are verdicts given one by one on finite samples. It does not state, and cannot obtain by finite enumeration, a conclusion over “a class of members × all subsequent inputs.” This paper holds universally for parameter families given by explicit rational conditions and for subsequent input of any length, any letters and any nonnegative rational teaching current; the read-back step uses the proved lemmas of that paper word for word (with versioned citation), and the load-bearing new content is the universal dynamical part: Lemmas 14.4, 14.5, 14.7 and the exact thresholds and equivalence characterization of Propositions 14-2, 14-3, 14-6 and Theorem 14-7, none of which is in that paper. The two are complementary: that paper gives the decision procedure, this paper the conditions under which qualification cannot fail to occur.  
　(2) Relative to Proposition 1 of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*: Proposition 1 is a necessary and sufficient closed form for the linear F families, a single history shape (formation + m rest beats + one drive beat) and a single window beat, and its HistCond is supplied directly by linear laws or relaxation that read their own position. Instances in 𝒦\_LS that satisfy the antecedents of Proposition N1 or N2 of Chapter 11 (such as Instance P) cannot be faithfully encoded within the encoding class E1–E4 of Chapter 11 (Propositions N1, N2; 3.6 of Chapter 11 states explicitly that it does not prove every member of 𝒦\_g unencodable, and unencodability of the whole class 𝒦\_LS is not proved either; the antecedent reading listed in 3.6 of Chapter 11 for beat 26 of Instance P is J/ε ≈ 6.256975 ∉ ℤ); for these instances neither Proposition 1 nor the F-layer read-back of Theorem 5 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* applies; for the other members, even if a faithful F encoding exists its antecedents must be checked instance by instance, and Theorem 14-1⁺ depends on no F encoding; conditions of the Proposition 1 type (formation, retention, an effective write in the window) are not sufficient on 𝒦\_LS (Appendix B.2 S5 D-K3′: formation effective, retention at window end, an effective write in the window, yet HDyn is the tier (b) 0\[B\_FD\]); the universal part of Theorem 14-1⁺ rests on invariants and induction, is independent of history length, and is not a generalization of any single-history closed form.  
　(3) Exclusion of shallowing components: C\_LS⁺ contains S3 (ρ = 0). Definition 3 of Chapter 11 states that potentiation and depression do not read w and that relaxation reads only the w at its own position. Hence, when ρ = 0, the only process acting on T that reads the w at its own position is clipping (which occurs only at saturation and is not used as the theorem's witness); within the law text of Definitions 1–3 of Chapter 11, the non-trivial route of HistCond passes only through the gate (this statement concerns only 𝒦\_g with ρ = 0 and does not extend to other host classes): the value of T enters the gating argument vt\_j, and the gate decides the occurrence of PLUS. HDyn can therefore be borne only by “the Trace-Response is a pivot for the gate,” and this does fail on concrete instances (baseline or residual membrane potential already above threshold: D-K3, D-K3′; Trace-Response insufficient to cross the threshold: D-F2; Trace-Response not yet available when the gate reads it: Instance N of Chapter 11). Relaxing to ρ \> 0, HDyn can be borne trivially by relaxation, while for ε \> 0 there is a long enough rest window on which retention fails; C chooses ρ = 0 so that universal retention over arbitrarily long windows and non-trivial HDyn hold at the same time.

7\. Boundary Cases and Conditions of Structural Collapse  
7.1  Conditions under which the conclusions fail (each item reports only the existence of a counterexample or the specific conclusion of the attached proof, and makes no general converse of the form “dropping some condition makes the conclusion fail”)  
　K3 fails: there is a legitimate trajectory on which the gate at the probe beat is open under every legitimate setting of T (Proposition 14-2, the conclusion carrying B\_FD; Appendix B.2 S5 D-K3, D-K3′, B.3 X4). With only the first clause of K3, the pivot fails on members with λ = 0 and Σ\_A b = θ (Note to Lemma 14.5 in 5.3; B.2 S3b, B.3 X5).  
　K2′ fails (d\_x \< 1): there is a legitimate trajectory on which T falls to z ≤ ε at window end (Proposition 14-3; B.2 S5 D-K2, S8); the same member can still satisfy R on other trajectories (B.2 S6).  
　The formation-amount condition fails: for a given g with Φ(g) \< θ there is a legitimate continuation on which R(T) fails (Proposition 14-6(2); B.2 S5 D-F2, B.8 D4); when F2 fails but Φ(g) ≥ θ, R still holds (the n = 4 example of B.2 S6).  
　ρ \> 0: the counterexample given has ε = 1/100, ρ = 1/10 and 60 rest beats after the probe, with z = 0.000125 ≤ ε at window end (B.2 S5 D-S3). The only general statement issued: on rest beats the only event acting on T is RELAX, z′ = (1 − ρ)z (without clipping), so for ε \> 0 and 0 \< ρ ≤ 1 there is a finite rest length with z ≤ ε; for ε = 0 and 0 \< ρ \< 1, z at the end of every finite window remains strictly positive, and failure of retention cannot be inferred from ρ \> 0.  
　A-decomposable alphabet: there is a legitimate trajectory on which single-entry letters depress without firing and T is eroded (B.2 S5 D-S4).  
　No certificate probe in W: Theorem 14-1⁺ does not apply; this implies neither the absence of events nor failure of qualification. Only on the concrete history with W all rest (B.2 S5 D-W) is there “no event on T”: no entry in W (no MINUS), I\_j = 0 and v\_j \< θ\_j so no firing (no PLUS), ρ = 0 (no RELAX), w unchanged (no CLIP) (B.7 V4).  
　F3ᵤ fails: the source criterion of this paper yields no r witness, recorded as ⊥, not 0; under S4, when a blocking unit exists, the first pairing beat has no M1₀ removal (Proposition 14-5(2); for decomposable alphabets this conclusion does not hold, B.10 G2; explicit example in B.7 V2, B.8 D3).  
　Clipping saturated and NS\* failing: the two readings disagree (⊥\_Ind), and H\_IND is required (Proposition 14-4(2); B.6 X6, B.8 D2).  
　d\_x = 1: the erosion construction of Proposition 14-3 fails, and the necessity of K2′ is no longer given by that construction (B.8 D5; U14-9).  
　Non-neural physical readings, biological neural tissue, neuromorphic hardware: not within the theorems of this paper (the prohibited read-backs of 7.3 of Chapter 11 apply equally).

### 7.2  H Group

　H\_IND (E11): consumed only when NS\* fails at every certificate probe in W; its root is the unanswered question of Section 8 of *The General Form of Field Concretization* and U6 of Chapter 11.  
　B\_FD\[𝒟₁\] (E10): consumed only in Proposition 14-2 and in the K3 direction of Theorem 14-7; Theorem 14-1⁺ does not consume it.  
　Read-back antecedents for the gated extension class, B\_η^PA and B\_r^PA: not consumed by Theorem 14-9, whose conclusion therefore reaches only the FD level; reading back R requires both.  
　The resource antecedent of Family 1ᵍ (a lower bound on the initial store): consumed only when Family 1ᵍ is read as physically valid.  
　The constitutive antecedents of the linear families, C1–C5, C4′, N1–N5: consumed only in Lemma 14-L; they are phenomenological antecedents declared by *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*.

### 7.3  Breach Modes

　K14-1  Give a member satisfying C\_LS⁺ (the full table of 4.3), a legitimate history and a window containing a certificate probe on which one of TraceSet, Part, HDyn fails on T (when NS\* holds, ⊥\_Ind may not serve as such).  
　K14-2  The antecedents of Proposition 14-2 hold, yet on every legitimate window containing an m-clean probe the gate at the probe beat closes under some legitimate setting; or the antecedents of Proposition 14-3 hold and K2′ fails, yet T is nondecreasing on every legitimate history.  
　K14-3  F3ᵤ holds, yet some legitimate prefix leaves the first pairing beat with no M1₀ removal; or the antecedents of Proposition 14-5(2) (S4 included) hold, yet an M1₀ removal exists.  
　K14-4  The antecedents of Proposition 14-4(2) hold, yet an effective joint net increment occurs in W.  
　K14-5  For a given g, Φ(g) ≥ θ (with the remaining conditions), yet R fails on some legitimate continuation; or Φ(g) \< θ, yet R holds on every legitimate continuation.  
　K14-6  The upper bound or strictness flag of Lemma 14.7 is violated on some legitimate trajectory; or at some certificate probe the gate stays open after T is set to b.  
　K14-7  C\_PA holds, yet one of (1)–(6) of Theorem 14-9 fails on some legitimate trajectory.  
　K14-8  The declared reading of Proposition 14-8 holds, yet for some length L the probability of containing an m-clean probe is below the stated lower bound.  
　Not breaches: C\_LS⁺ failing; no certificate probe in W (the theorem does not apply); NS\* failing with E11 not declared; the ⊥\_Over of Proposition 14-2 when B\_FD is not declared; Theorem 14-9 not reading back to HistCond (its strength is FD level by design); failure of the budget of Family 1ᵍ when the resource antecedent is not declared; disagreement between the F layer and the mechanism layer when L5′ of Lemma 14-L fails (a fidelity failure; the formal R may still be 1, and it is not a negative determination of qualification); failure of the blocking test of Proposition 14-5(2) on decomposable alphabets (the antecedent S4 is not met); experimental failures in 9.1 when not all the conditions used have been measured or controlled; readings on real hosts.

8\. Model-Layer Witnesses and Finite Verification (Column A; Appendix B)  
The programs give finite verification, explicit witnesses and counterexamples; random generation covers only its generating domain; universal conclusions rest on the proofs of Sections 5 and 6. “R witness = 1” means that verify in Appendix A.1 constructs positive witnesses for q, r, Part and HDyn under the atom criteria of Chapter 11; the verdicts of the decision procedure of Chapter 11 (Appendix A.3–A.8) are instance-by-instance verdicts, serve as cross-checks, and do not enter the proofs. check\_C in Appendix A.1 checks the coefficient part of C\_LS⁺ with the formation-amount condition in form (i), and its K2 item is the “d\_y \< 1” branch of K2′; A.2 additionally checks the A₋ = 0 branch of K2′, F3\*, F3°, F2′, Φ and certificate probes.

9\. Real Hosts: Conditional Predictions (Column B) and Evidence Register (Column C)  
9.1  Human hippocampal and human neocortical STDP synapse populations (evidence cards: Testa-Silva et al. 2010, Verhoog et al. 2013; the facts on the cards have not been independently verified)  
　Object of prediction: the dynamical part of Theorem 14-1⁺—self-sustainment (Lemma 14.4) and the probe pivot (Lemma 14.5)—predicted under the full model and protocol conditions. The conditions used are the following items of 4.3; if any one is missing, the case is outside the prediction. Each item notes whether it must be measured or controlled:  
　　Form of the law: the plasticity law can be written as a trace-based pair rule of 𝒦\_g type (Definitions 1–3 of Chapter 11: potentiation by the presynaptic eligibility trace, depression by the postsynaptic firing trace, firing as threshold gating; the broad time window and the reversal at −80 ms in the cited experiments differ from this in form and remain to be verified). Must be measured.
